### MERIT: Maximum-normalized Element-wise Ratio for Language Model Large-batch Training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20577v1)
- **Authors**: Yang Luo, Zangwei Zheng, Ziheng Qin, Zirui Zhu, Yong Liu, Yang You
- **Abstract**: Large-batch training has become a cornerstone in accelerating the training of deep neural networks, yet it poses challenges in optimization and generalization. Existing optimizers like AdamW present performance degradation during language models' large-batch training, due to the information bottleneck in attention layers caused by the sharp increase of max attention logit. While the LAMB optimizer partially addresses this issue, some attention layers still face this issue. The reason is that $l_2$-norm-based trust ratios in LAMB are less effective in directly influencing the max value of query/key weights. Furthermore, the weight-wise trust ratio in LAMB is error-prone as it overlooks relationships of weight values within rows or columns. Building on these observations, we propose a novel optimizer, MERIT, which leverages the max-norm to calculate the trust ratio to constrain the max attention logit more effectively. Moreover, we further construct element-wise trust ratios to provide more robust update scaling by focusing on local weight structures. Extensive experiments of large-batch training across various sizes of GPT-2 models demonstrate the superior performance of MERIT. Notably, during the training of GPT-2 Medium, MERIT enables a 6k batch size without any performance degradation compared to the standard batch size (480) with 48B training tokens. This work highlights the importance of considering the max attention logit and finer-granularity trust ratio in large-batch training. It successfully improves the training stability and paves the way for larger batch usage, enabling faster development and iteration of large language models. Code is available at https://github.com/NUS-HPC-AI-Lab/MERIT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MERIT: Maximum-normalized Element-wise Ratio for Language Model Large-batch Training":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MERIT, a novel optimizer designed for large-batch training of language models. It addresses the performance degradation observed with optimizers like AdamW during large-batch training, which the authors attribute to an information bottleneck in attention layers caused by a sharp increase in the maximum attention logit. MERIT leverages max-norm to calculate a trust ratio, aiming to constrain the max attention logit more effectively. It also constructs element-wise trust ratios to provide more robust update scaling by focusing on local weight structures. Experimental results on GPT-2 models demonstrate improved performance with MERIT, enabling larger batch sizes without significant performance degradation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the combination of max-norm based trust ratios with element-wise refinement for large-batch training. The authors build upon the existing LAMB optimizer but identify and address its limitations, particularly its inefficiency in preventing the max attention logit from spiking and the error-prone nature of its weight-wise trust ratio. Using max-norm specifically for attention weights is reasonable given the known issues of attention heads becoming dominant. The element-wise ratio is motivated and executed well.

*   **Significance:** Large-batch training is crucial for scaling language model training, but it introduces optimization challenges. The paper contributes by offering a method (MERIT) that can effectively scale to larger batch sizes while maintaining or improving model performance. This has practical implications for faster development and iteration of large language models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Identification:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the problem of max attention logit increase in large-batch training with AdamW.
    *   **Technical Approach:** The proposed MERIT optimizer is well-motivated and technically sound. The combination of max-norm and element-wise trust ratios seems effective.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The experiments across different GPT-2 model sizes provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of MERIT. The ablation studies further support the importance of individual components of the optimizer.
    *   **Analysis:** The analysis of Hessian matrices and convergence behavior adds more evidence to the performance of MERIT.
    *   **Comparison:** The comparisons include strong optimizers such as Sophia-G and LION in addition to AdamW and LAMB which is a strong move.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Model Variety:** The experimental evaluation is primarily based on the GPT-2 architecture. While GPT-2 is a widely used model, further validation on other transformer variants (e.g., BERT, newer architectures) would strengthen the claims of general applicability. Additional results on Llama are helpful, but aren't as thorough as those performed on GPT2.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper claims minimal extra computational overhead, a more detailed breakdown of the computational cost associated with max-norm calculations and element-wise ratios would be beneficial. The table showing time taken has other optimizations that could also be factored into wall time such as CUDA kernel versions that aren't strictly related to the optimizer. The increase in FLOPs is negligible, however.
    *   **Scaling Laws:** It is worth noting that while the experiments are conducted on models that attempt to follow the Chinchilla Scaling Law, the models are too small to truly make a strong claims that they are well scaled compared to larger models.
    *   **Clarity of Convergence Analysis:** The convergence analysis in the appendix is somewhat dense and could benefit from more intuitive explanations and illustrations.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research on large-batch training optimization. MERIT could be adopted as a baseline optimizer in future studies and inspire further work on adaptive learning rate methods that consider the max attention logit and local weight structures. The ideas of scaling to large batch size can lead to faster training and experimentation on larger models.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the paper presents a novel and effective optimizer, the limitations in model variety and clarity prevent it from receiving a higher score. There is a clear contribution and significant empirical improvement in performance.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Graph Talks, But Who's Listening? Rethinking Evaluations for Graph-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20583v1)
- **Authors**: Soham Petkar, Hari Aakash K, Anirudh Vempati, Akshit Sinha, Ponnurangam Kumarauguru, Chirag Agarwal
- **Abstract**: Developments in Graph-Language Models (GLMs) aim to integrate the structural reasoning capabilities of Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) with the semantic understanding of Large Language Models (LLMs). However, we demonstrate that current evaluation benchmarks for GLMs, which are primarily repurposed node-level classification datasets, are insufficient to assess multimodal reasoning. Our analysis reveals that strong performance on these benchmarks is achievable using unimodal information alone, suggesting that they do not necessitate graph-language integration. To address this evaluation gap, we introduce the CLEGR(Compositional Language-Graph Reasoning) benchmark, designed to evaluate multimodal reasoning at various complexity levels. Our benchmark employs a synthetic graph generation pipeline paired with questions that require joint reasoning over structure and textual semantics. We perform a thorough evaluation of representative GLM architectures and find that soft-prompted LLM baselines perform on par with GLMs that incorporate a full GNN backbone. This result calls into question the architectural necessity of incorporating graph structure into LLMs. We further show that GLMs exhibit significant performance degradation in tasks that require structural reasoning. These findings highlight limitations in the graph reasoning capabilities of current GLMs and provide a foundation for advancing the community toward explicit multimodal reasoning involving graph structure and language.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "A Graph Talks, But Who's Listening? Rethinking Evaluations for Graph-Language Models."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses a critical gap in the evaluation of Graph-Language Models (GLMs). The authors argue that existing benchmarks, primarily node-level classification tasks, are insufficient to assess the multimodal reasoning capabilities that GLMs are intended to provide. Their analysis reveals that strong performance on these benchmarks can be achieved using unimodal information alone (either graph structure or textual content), negating the need for genuine graph-language integration. To rectify this, the paper introduces CLEGR (Compositional Language-Graph Reasoning), a new benchmark designed to evaluate multimodal reasoning at various complexity levels. CLEGR employs a synthetic graph generation pipeline paired with questions requiring joint reasoning over both structure and textual semantics. The authors' experiments using representative GLM architectures demonstrate that soft-prompted LLMs perform comparably to GLMs incorporating full GNN backbones, casting doubt on the architectural necessity of integrating graph structure. They also find that GLMs exhibit performance degradation in tasks requiring structural reasoning, underscoring limitations in current GLM capabilities. The paper concludes by highlighting the need for advancing the community toward explicit multimodal reasoning involving graph structure and language.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant argument that current evaluation paradigms for GLMs are flawed and misleading. While there have been critiques of benchmarks in other domains, this work specifically targets the evaluation methods in graph-language modeling, offering a compelling case based on both analysis of existing benchmarks and the introduction of a new benchmark. The CLEGR dataset itself is a novel contribution.
*   **Significance:** The findings have substantial implications for the field. By showing that existing GLMs often don't fully leverage both graph and language modalities, the paper challenges the current architectural trends and encourages researchers to rethink how these models are designed and evaluated. The CLEGR benchmark provides a more rigorous testbed for future GLM development. This has significant potential to shape the direction of research in this area.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the limitations of existing GLM evaluation methods.
    *   **Empirical Evidence:** The authors provide strong empirical evidence to support their claims, including experiments with unimodal baselines and linear probing analysis.
    *   **Novel Benchmark:** The introduction of CLEGR is a valuable contribution, offering a more appropriate and challenging evaluation platform. The design of CLEGR is well-motivated by the identified shortcomings of existing benchmarks.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation:** The experiments on CLEGR are comprehensive, covering various GLM architectures and reasoning tasks.
    *   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, making the arguments and findings accessible.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Data:** CLEGR is a synthetic dataset. While this helps avoid pre-training confounds, there is a risk that the skills learned on CLEGR may not fully transfer to real-world graph-language reasoning scenarios. Further evaluation on real-world datasets is necessary to fully assess the generalizability of future GLMs developed using CLEGR.
    *   **Limited Scope of Architectures:** The paper focuses on a few representative GLM architectures, specifically those with LLMs as predictors. It could benefit from a broader exploration of architectures, particularly those where LLMs act as encoders or aligners.
    *   **Potential LLM fine-tuning differences:** There is a risk of subtle differences between soft prompted LLMs and GLMs fine-tuning, which can make GLMs more prone to forgetting, while soft prompting is robust.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has a high potential to influence the field by:

    *   Shifting the focus of evaluation towards more rigorous multimodal reasoning.
    *   Inspiring the development of new GLM architectures that truly integrate graph and language information.
    *   Encouraging the creation of more diverse and challenging graph-language benchmarks.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8** to this paper. The paper makes a convincing argument and provides strong evidence of the limitations of existing GLM evaluation practices. The introduction of CLEGR addresses a critical gap in the field, and the authors' experiments reveal important insights about the capabilities (and limitations) of current GLMs. The paper has significant potential to shape future research in graph-language modeling. The primary weakness lies in the synthetic nature of the CLEGR dataset and the limited scope of the architectures examined. However, these limitations do not diminish the paper's overall contribution and impact. The paper's rigorous methodology and novel benchmark substantially advance our understanding of graph-language reasoning.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### FastFit: Accelerating Multi-Reference Virtual Try-On via Cacheable Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20586v1)
- **Authors**: Zheng Chong, Yanwei Lei, Shiyue Zhang, Zhuandi He, Zhen Wang, Xujie Zhang, Xiao Dong, Yiling Wu, Dongmei Jiang, Xiaodan Liang
- **Abstract**: Despite its great potential, virtual try-on technology is hindered from real-world application by two major challenges: the inability of current methods to support multi-reference outfit compositions (including garments and accessories), and their significant inefficiency caused by the redundant re-computation of reference features in each denoising step. To address these challenges, we propose FastFit, a high-speed multi-reference virtual try-on framework based on a novel cacheable diffusion architecture. By employing a Semi-Attention mechanism and substituting traditional timestep embeddings with class embeddings for reference items, our model fully decouples reference feature encoding from the denoising process with negligible parameter overhead. This allows reference features to be computed only once and losslessly reused across all steps, fundamentally breaking the efficiency bottleneck and achieving an average 3.5x speedup over comparable methods. Furthermore, to facilitate research on complex, multi-reference virtual try-on, we introduce DressCode-MR, a new large-scale dataset. It comprises 28,179 sets of high-quality, paired images covering five key categories (tops, bottoms, dresses, shoes, and bags), constructed through a pipeline of expert models and human feedback refinement. Extensive experiments on the VITON-HD, DressCode, and our DressCode-MR datasets show that FastFit surpasses state-of-the-art methods on key fidelity metrics while offering its significant advantage in inference efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FastFit: Accelerating Multi-Reference Virtual Try-On via Cacheable Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FastFit, a novel framework for high-speed multi-reference virtual try-on, addressing the limitations of existing methods in handling multiple garments and accessories simultaneously and their computational inefficiency. FastFit utilizes a cacheable diffusion architecture that decouples reference feature encoding from the iterative denoising process, allowing for features to be computed once and reused across steps. This is achieved through a Reference Class Embedding and a Semi-Attention mechanism. Furthermore, the paper presents DressCode-MR, a new large-scale dataset for multi-reference try-on, covering five key categories: tops, bottoms, dresses, shoes, and bags. Experimental results on multiple datasets demonstrate that FastFit outperforms state-of-the-art methods in terms of fidelity and inference speed.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the *cacheable diffusion architecture*.  Decoupling the reference feature encoding from the denoising process is a significant contribution to the efficiency of virtual try-on. The Reference Class Embedding and Semi-Attention mechanism are clever solutions that enable this decoupling. Without this approach, there were two problematic options:  (1) ReferenceNet, which significantly increases the number of model parameters, or (2) in-context learning, which means redundant and repeated computation for features at each denoising step.  The creation of the DressCode-MR dataset, while valuable, is a supporting contribution rather than the primary novelty.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its ability to *accelerate the virtual try-on process* without sacrificing fidelity.  The 3.5x speedup is a substantial improvement, making multi-reference virtual try-on more practical. This addresses a key bottleneck that previously hindered real-world applications. Also, making the virtual try-on process support multiple reference items simultaneously, to be combined into coherent visual output is a novel advancement.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing methods and motivates the need for a more efficient and versatile solution.
    *   **Technically Sound:** The proposed architecture and methods are well-explained and justified.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of FastFit across multiple datasets and metrics, showcasing improvements in both fidelity and efficiency. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component.
    *   **Valuable Dataset:** DressCode-MR addresses a gap in available resources for multi-reference try-on research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Discussion of Limitations:** While the paper highlights the strong performance of FastFit, it doesn't deeply explore potential failure cases or scenarios where the method might struggle.  For instance, there's not enough discussion about complex physical interactions or clothing that are hard to model with diffusion models.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The individual components, while clever in combination, are not revolutionary in isolation. Diffusion models, attention mechanisms, and conditioning techniques are well-established. The innovation is in how they are combined and optimized specifically for the multi-reference try-on task.
*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of virtual try-on by making it more practical and accessible. Faster inference times can be critical for real-time or interactive applications. The DressCode-MR dataset can also facilitate further research in this area. The introduction of class embeddings to reference features is a novel approach that could be implemented in other methods. However, real-world adoption will also depend on factors outside the algorithm itself, such as user adoption, rendering realistic clothing textures, support of different human body types, etc.
* **Reasons for score given:** FastFit offers a novel, efficient, and high-fidelity solution to multi-reference virtual try-on by introducing class embedding and a new attention mechanism, which significantly reduces computation time and memory requirements while preserving high-quality results, it contributes to making virtual try-on more practical, and its accompanying dataset is also valuable. The score reflects FastFit's novelty and contribution, and also the limited discussion of limitations.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### SemSR: Semantics aware robust Session-based Recommendations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20587v1)
- **Authors**: Jyoti Narwariya, Priyanka Gupta, Muskan Gupta, Jyotsana Khatri, Lovekesh Vig
- **Abstract**: Session-based recommendation (SR) models aim to recommend items to anonymous users based on their behavior during the current session. While various SR models in the literature utilize item sequences to predict the next item, they often fail to leverage semantic information from item titles or descriptions impeding session intent identification and interpretability. Recent research has explored Large Language Models (LLMs) as promising approaches to enhance session-based recommendations, with both prompt-based and fine-tuning based methods being widely investigated. However, prompt-based methods struggle to identify optimal prompts that elicit correct reasoning and lack task-specific feedback at test time, resulting in sub-optimal recommendations. Fine-tuning methods incorporate domain-specific knowledge but incur significant computational costs for implementation and maintenance. In this paper, we present multiple approaches to utilize LLMs for session-based recommendation: (i) in-context LLMs as recommendation agents, (ii) LLM-generated representations for semantic initialization of deep learning SR models, and (iii) integration of LLMs with data-driven SR models. Through comprehensive experiments on two real-world publicly available datasets, we demonstrate that LLM-based methods excel at coarse-level retrieval (high recall values), while traditional data-driven techniques perform well at fine-grained ranking (high Mean Reciprocal Rank values). Furthermore, the integration of LLMs with data-driven SR models significantly out performs both standalone LLM approaches and data-driven deep learning models, as well as baseline SR models, in terms of both Recall and MRR metrics.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary**

The paper "SemSR: Semantics aware robust Session-based Recommendations" explores methods to enhance session-based recommendation (SR) models by incorporating Large Language Models (LLMs). The paper investigates three main approaches: (1) using in-context LLMs directly as recommendation agents, (2) leveraging LLMs to generate semantic item representations for initializing deep learning SR models, and (3) integrating LLMs with data-driven SR models. The authors demonstrate that while LLMs excel at coarse-level retrieval (high recall), traditional data-driven methods perform better at fine-grained ranking (high Mean Reciprocal Rank). The integration of LLMs with data-driven SR models outperforms both standalone LLM approaches and data-driven deep learning models. Experiments are conducted on two publicly available datasets. The authors test their approaches on MSGAT and NISER models.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic exploration of several approaches to integrate LLMs into session-based recommendation. While other papers have considered LLMs for recommendation, this work specifically examines: 1) in-context learning with LLMs, 2) semantic initialization, and 3) the fusion of LLM embeddings with data-driven models in a single framework. It also evaluates different LLM prompting methods such as few-shot learning, zero-shot chain-of-thought, and few-shot chain-of-thought. In addition, unlike other methods, the entire process of learning is end-to-end. While each integration method in isolation might have been touched upon in other works, the combination of these approaches and the empirical analysis provides a relatively novel contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its practical insights into how LLMs can be used effectively in session-based recommendation. The finding that LLMs are good at coarse-level retrieval but not fine-grained ranking is particularly important. This result informs how LLMs can best be combined with traditional recommendation techniques. The superior performance of integrated models suggests a promising direction for future research. The comprehensive experiments on two real-world datasets add to the robustness of the results. However, testing on more datasets would strengthen the paper's findings.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated research questions.
    *   Systematic exploration of different LLM integration methods.
    *   Comprehensive experiments with appropriate evaluation metrics (Recall and MRR).
    *   Well-written and clearly explained.
    *   The paper identifies specific benefits associated with LLM representations such as semantic awareness.
    *   The paper provides solid ablative studies and experimental comparisons against a rich set of baselines including traditional approaches as well as recent works leveraging LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper's experimental analysis is limited to two datasets.
    *   The specific LLM used (Llama3) is relatively recent, and the generalizability of the results to other LLMs needs further investigation. While using the very latest LLMs can add a point of novelty, it can also introduce an element of instability for experimental results.
    *   Although the approach is model-agnostic, the models tested on (MSGAT and NISER) are slightly dated compared to the state-of-the-art. Testing more contemporary models would enhance the significance of the findings.
    *   The gains over data-driven methods with LLMs is not significant and could be further enhanced by focusing on optimizing LLM representation and integration techniques.
    *   There is limited qualitative analysis or discussion of specific recommendation examples. Understanding *why* the integrated models perform better would strengthen the paper.
    *   Computational costs are not discussed in detail which is important when using LLMs.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in session-based recommendation by guiding researchers towards hybrid approaches that combine the strengths of LLMs and traditional data-driven methods. The findings regarding the coarse-level retrieval capabilities of LLMs and the need for fine-grained ranking using other techniques could inspire new model architectures and training strategies.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a valuable and systematic exploration of LLM integration in session-based recommendation, highlighting key strengths and weaknesses of LLMs in this context. The systematic exploration and clear presentation are definite strengths. However, limitations in the experimental scope (only two datasets, slightly older SR models), lack of deeper qualitative analysis, and only incremental gains over existing methods prevent it from achieving a higher score. A score of 6 reflects that this is a solid, incremental contribution to the field, with potential for future impact if the findings are further validated and expanded upon.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Disruptive Attacks on Face Swapping via Low-Frequency Perceptual Perturbations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20595v1)
- **Authors**: Mengxiao Huang, Minglei Shu, Shuwang Zhou, Zhaoyang Liu
- **Abstract**: Deepfake technology, driven by Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs), poses significant risks to privacy and societal security. Existing detection methods are predominantly passive, focusing on post-event analysis without preventing attacks. To address this, we propose an active defense method based on low-frequency perceptual perturbations to disrupt face swapping manipulation, reducing the performance and naturalness of generated content. Unlike prior approaches that used low-frequency perturbations to impact classification accuracy,our method directly targets the generative process of deepfake techniques. We combine frequency and spatial domain features to strengthen defenses. By introducing artifacts through low-frequency perturbations while preserving high-frequency details, we ensure the output remains visually plausible. Additionally, we design a complete architecture featuring an encoder, a perturbation generator, and a decoder, leveraging discrete wavelet transform (DWT) to extract low-frequency components and generate perturbations that disrupt facial manipulation models. Experiments on CelebA-HQ and LFW demonstrate significant reductions in face-swapping effectiveness, improved defense success rates, and preservation of visual quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes an active defense method against face-swapping attacks using low-frequency perceptual perturbations. The core idea is to add imperceptible noise in the frequency domain to disrupt the generative process of deepfake techniques, specifically face-swapping models. This is achieved through an encoder-perturbation generator-decoder architecture, utilizing Discrete Wavelet Transform (DWT) to separate frequency components and generate targeted perturbations. The method aims to preserve visual quality while reducing the effectiveness and naturalness of face-swapped content. Experimental results on CelebA-HQ and LFW datasets demonstrate significant reductions in face-swapping success rates and improved defense performance compared to existing methods, while maintaining visual plausibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its targeted approach to disrupting face-swapping *generation* using low-frequency perturbations. While low-frequency perturbations have been used for other adversarial purposes (e.g., affecting classification), the direct targeting of the generative process in face-swapping is a distinct contribution.  The architecture, incorporating DWT and SE-ResNet within the encoder-decoder structure, also adds incremental novelty by combining existing techniques in a specific and potentially effective way. Prior work like CMUA have used perturbation based defenses, however, they lacked sufficient effectiveness against sophisticated face swapping. This work improves upon prior work specifically using perturbations in the frequency domain to avoid visual degradation.

* **Significance:** The research addresses a timely and important problem – the malicious use of deepfake technology. Active defense mechanisms are crucial in preventing deepfakes rather than simply detecting them after the fact. The results show a significant improvement in defense success rates compared to several existing methods. The emphasis on preserving visual quality is also important; defenses that severely degrade image quality are less likely to be adopted in practice. However, the reliance on specific datasets and face-swapping architectures limits the generalizability of conclusions.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Targeted approach:** Directly addresses the generative process of face-swapping models.
    * **Visual quality:** Successfully balances defense performance with visual plausibility.
    * **Comprehensive architecture:** Integrates various components for feature extraction, perturbation generation, and image reconstruction.
    * **Quantitative results:** Demonstrates improved defense success rates and visual quality compared to existing methods.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Generalizability:** The evaluation is primarily focused on CelebA-HQ and LFW datasets and may not generalize well to other datasets or face-swapping models. The paper should thoroughly analyze the model's performance against a broader range of architectures and in more realistic, compressed online environments. The lack of comparison with the 2023 models is also a minor drawback as stated by the authors.
    * **Perturbation detectability:** While the paper emphasizes imperceptibility, a more thorough analysis of the detectability of the perturbations is needed. If the perturbations are easily detected by other defense mechanisms, the effectiveness of the approach could be compromised.
    * **Defensive robustness:** There is no analysis regarding the robustness of the proposed defense against adaptive attacks. An attacker could potentially design an attack that is specifically tailored to circumvent the proposed defense by, for example, learning to remove the low-frequency perturbation during generation.
    * **Incremental novelty:** Some components, while well integrated, are adaptations of existing techniques.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in active defense against deepfakes. The concept of targeting generative processes using frequency-domain perturbations is a promising direction. Further research could explore the robustness of this approach against adaptive attacks, its generalizability to different datasets and architectures, and its applicability to other deepfake generation techniques (e.g., facial reenactment, attribute manipulation).

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:**  The paper presents a valuable contribution by directly addressing the generative process of face-swapping models through low-frequency perturbation, resulting in a measurable improvement in defense performance. However, the lack of thorough robustness analysis, limited generalizability testing (especially in realistic compression environments), and reliance on specific datasets/architectures temper the overall impact. While the paper's idea is innovative, further extensive testing to assess robustness and generalizability would be needed to score it significantly higher.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Physics Informed Generative Models for Magnetic Field Images
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20612v1)
- **Authors**: Aye Phyu Phyu Aung, Lucas Lum, Zhansen Shi, Wen Qiu, Bernice Zee, JM Chin, Yeow Kheng Lim, J. Senthilnath
- **Abstract**: In semiconductor manufacturing, defect detection and localization are critical to ensuring product quality and yield. While X-ray imaging is a reliable non-destructive testing method, it is memory-intensive and time-consuming for large-scale scanning, Magnetic Field Imaging (MFI) offers a more efficient means to localize regions of interest (ROI) for targeted X-ray scanning. However, the limited availability of MFI datasets due to proprietary concerns presents a significant bottleneck for training machine learning (ML) models using MFI. To address this challenge, we consider an ML-driven approach leveraging diffusion models with two physical constraints. We propose Physics Informed Generative Models for Magnetic Field Images (PI-GenMFI) to generate synthetic MFI samples by integrating specific physical information. We generate MFI images for the most common defect types: power shorts. These synthetic images will serve as training data for ML algorithms designed to localize defect areas efficiently. To evaluate generated MFIs, we compare our model to SOTA generative models from both variational autoencoder (VAE) and diffusion methods. We present a domain expert evaluation to assess the generated samples. In addition, we present qualitative and quantitative evaluation using various metrics used for image generation and signal processing, showing promising results to optimize the defect localization process.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of limited availability of Magnetic Field Imaging (MFI) datasets for training machine learning (ML) models used in semiconductor defect localization.  It proposes Physics Informed Generative Models for Magnetic Field Images (PI-GenMFI) to generate synthetic MFI samples.  The approach integrates physical constraints derived from Maxwell's equations and Ampere's Law into a diffusion model to ensure the generated images respect electromagnetic field principles. The paper evaluates the generated MFIs against SOTA generative models (VAE and diffusion) using qualitative (domain expert evaluation) and quantitative metrics, showing promising results in optimizing the defect localization process. They demonstrate improvements with their method over vanilla diffusion models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the specific application of physics-informed generative models to the domain of magnetic field image synthesis for semiconductor failure analysis.  While physics-informed machine learning and generative models are established concepts, their combination and targeted application to MFI datasets for addressing the data scarcity problem is a unique contribution. The two physical constraints chosen are well-justified and relevant to the problem. Furthermore, the paper appears to be the first to propose generating MFI data for ML in non-destructive failure analysis.

* **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to alleviate a major bottleneck in applying ML techniques to semiconductor defect localization.  The limited availability of real MFI data hinders the development and deployment of data-driven methods for failure analysis. By providing a means to generate synthetic data, PI-GenMFI could enable the development of more robust and accurate defect localization algorithms, ultimately improving product quality and yield in semiconductor manufacturing.  The work offers a practical solution to a real-world problem faced in the semiconductor industry. However, the small size of the initial dataset (17 samples) might limit the diversity and generalizability of the generated data. The paper could have benefited from a detailed exploration of the limitations of the generated data.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Problem Addressed:** Targets a significant issue in a crucial industry.
    * **Methodology:** Combines relevant techniques (diffusion models, physics constraints) effectively.
    * **Evaluation:** Uses a combination of quantitative metrics and domain expert validation.
    * **Clarity:** The paper is well-structured and easy to follow.
    * **Justification for design choices:** The reasoning for the specific constraints and DDPM are well laid out.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Real Data:**  The model relies on a very small set of real MFI samples (17), raising concerns about the representativeness and diversity of the generated data. More justification of its usefulness would be useful.
    * **FID reliability**: The justification of FID's unreliability is only weakly defended.
    * **Limited Defect Type**: The analysis is limited to one defect type (power shorts). The model's applicability to other defect types needs to be demonstrated.
    * **Domain Expert Evaluation:** Only one expert gave it an acceptable score (Expert 1). More information on the reasoning for their different opinions might enhance the paper.
    * **Lack of downstream task result:** The paper would greatly benefit from using the generated dataset in a downstream ML task to improve defect localization. The ultimate goal of this work is to provide enough training samples to assist in defect localization, and this metric is absent.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in the area of semiconductor failure analysis. It could encourage other researchers to explore physics-informed generative models for data augmentation in other fields with limited datasets and where physical constraints are important. It also opens up opportunities for improving defect localization algorithms by leveraging synthetic MFI data. The idea is extensible to other sensor modalities and failure analysis techniques.

* **Rigorous Rationale:** The justification for a moderate score is driven by the strong potential, coupled with the limitations in data diversity and missing downstream results. The idea of physics-informed generation for this application is sound and tackles a practical problem.  However, the dependency on a very small real dataset and lack of demonstration in an actual defect localization task reduces the demonstrated impact of the work at this stage.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Schema-Guided Response Generation using Multi-Frame Dialogue State for Motivational Interviewing Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20635v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Zeng, Yukiko I. Nakano
- **Abstract**: The primary goal of Motivational Interviewing (MI) is to help clients build their own motivation for behavioral change. To support this in dialogue systems, it is essential to guide large language models (LLMs) to generate counselor responses aligned with MI principles. By employing a schema-guided approach, this study proposes a method for updating multi-frame dialogue states and a strategy decision mechanism that dynamically determines the response focus in a manner grounded in MI principles. The proposed method was implemented in a dialogue system and evaluated through a user study. Results showed that the proposed system successfully generated MI-favorable responses and effectively encouraged the user's (client's) deliberation by asking eliciting questions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, focusing on novelty, significance, and providing a justified score.

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of building dialogue systems for Motivational Interviewing (MI), focusing on generating MI-aligned counselor responses. The core contribution is a schema-guided approach using multi-frame dialogue state representation. This involves representing the dialogue state as a set of frames (Goal, Problem, Experience, Plan), updating this state with an LLM, and using the updated state and dialogue history to determine a suitable dialogue strategy (intent and focus). A strategy pool is created from an MI corpus and is used to select few-shot examples for guiding an LLM to generate the counselor's response. The system was evaluated through a user study, comparing it to baseline prompt-based systems (MI Few-shot and Principle-based Prompt Guide).  Results suggest the proposed system encourages user deliberation by asking eliciting questions and reduces the frequency of non-MI adherent behaviors compared to the baseline systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the application of a schema-guided approach (typically used in task-oriented dialogue) to the more open-ended domain of Motivational Interviewing. While existing research has explored prompt-based approaches for MI dialogue generation, the paper claims to improve response generation by enhancing dialogue state representation (using the multi-frame structure) and dialogue strategies derived from MI principles extracted from a dataset.

*   **Significance:** MI is an important technique for promoting behavior change, and automated MI systems have potential value. Improving the alignment of such systems with MI principles is a worthwhile goal. The paper shows potential for better response generation through structured state management and dynamic strategy decision. However, user ratings suggest that the proposed system may have been perceived as less empathetic than the baseline systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The multi-frame dialogue state representation provides a structured way to capture different aspects of the client's concerns (goals, problems, experiences, plans).
    *   The method for generating dialogue strategies by retrieving from a pseudo-strategy pool is grounded in MI knowledge.
    *   The user study provides evidence that the system asks eliciting questions and limits non-MI-adherent behavior.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The improvements are incremental to existing prompt-based methods. There's no drastic shift in performance.
    *   The dependence on LLMs means the system inherits their inherent biases and potential for generating inappropriate content. The ethics section acknowledges these limitations.
    *   The user study, while useful, is relatively small. The generalizability to different demographics and broader MI topics needs further investigation.
    *   The system's lower performance in Q1 (responding based on what you said and discussed), Q3 (asking about the user's current issues) and Q14 (motivating users to change through dialogue) suggest it might have prioritized eliciting thoughts at the expense of empathy and genuine engagement with the user's experiences.
    *   The limited set of predefined frame types and response intentions restrict the flexibility of the dialogue state, and potentially makes the system unable to handle some topics.
    *   The authors admit that, for certain items, the MI-FS and MI-Guide had significantly higher ratings than our proposed system, suggesting that MI-FS and MI-Guide were perceived as more active in offering ideas and suggestions.

*   **Justification for the Score:** The paper offers a reasonable contribution, but it doesn't represent a groundbreaking shift. The application of schema-guided methods to MI is a logical step, but the empirical improvements are moderate, and the user study highlights areas needing improvement, like empathy. The system’s structured approach and the use of a knowledge-based pool of strategies are beneficial, but the complexity of MI and the limitations of the LLM-based approach mean the system isn't a perfect solution. The fact that user rated the proposed system as *less* empathetic than competing systems (even if it reduced non-MI compliant responses) also diminishes its overall impact and potential for effective deployment.

- **Classification**: cs.HC

### GDS Agent: A Graph Algorithmic Reasoning Agent
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20637v1)
- **Authors**: Borun Shi, Ioannis Panagiotas
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown remarkable multimodal information processing and reasoning ability. When equipped with tools through function calling and enhanced with retrieval-augmented techniques, compound LLM-based systems can access closed data sources and answer questions about them. However, they still struggle to process and reason over large-scale graph-structure data. We introduce the GDS (Graph Data Science) agent in this technical report. The GDS agent introduces a comprehensive set of graph algorithms as tools, together with preprocessing (retrieval) and postprocessing of algorithm results, in a model context protocol (MCP) server. The server can be used with any modern LLM out-of-the-box. GDS agent allows users to ask any question that implicitly and intrinsically requires graph algorithmic reasoning about their data, and quickly obtain accurate and grounded answers. We also introduce a new benchmark that evaluates intermediate tool calls as well as final responses. The results indicate that GDS agent is able to solve a wide spectrum of graph tasks. We also provide detailed case studies for more open-ended tasks and study scenarios where the agent struggles. Finally, we discuss the remaining challenges and the future roadmap.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the GDS Agent paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the GDS (Graph Data Science) Agent, a system designed to enhance Large Language Model (LLM) capabilities for reasoning over large-scale graph-structured data. The GDS Agent leverages a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server containing graph algorithms as tools.  This setup allows LLMs to answer questions that intrinsically require graph algorithmic reasoning on real-world knowledge graphs. The authors also present a new benchmark designed to evaluate both intermediate tool calls and final responses of such agents. Results suggest the GDS agent can solve a wide range of graph-based tasks. Case studies explore more open-ended tasks, and the paper identifies scenarios where the agent struggles, discussing future challenges and roadmap.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of integrating graph algorithms as tools for LLMs to reason over graph data is moderately novel. Prior work has explored graph transformers and prompting LLMs with textualized graphs, but this work provides a more direct and scalable integration approach using existing graph data science libraries (like Neo4j GDS). The introduction of a benchmark is a positive contribution.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant limitation of LLMs: their difficulty in reasoning over structured graph data. By providing a mechanism for LLMs to leverage established graph algorithms, the GDS Agent opens the door for more effective use of LLMs in domains involving knowledge graphs, social networks, and other graph-based datasets. This could impact areas like fraud detection, recommendation systems, and network analysis.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Practical Approach:** The GDS Agent leverages existing infrastructure (LLMs and graph database libraries) making it a potentially practical solution.
    * **Comprehensive Toolset:** The inclusion of a comprehensive set of graph algorithms as tools is a significant strength.
    * **Benchmark Contribution:** The graph-agent-bench benchmark provides a valuable resource for evaluating graph reasoning capabilities of LLMs.
    * **Case Studies:** The case studies offer insights into the agent's behavior on more complex tasks and highlight both successes and failure points.
    * **Clear Architecture Explanation:** The explanation of the MCP server and its interaction with the LLM is well-presented.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited LLM Reasoning Evaluation:** While the benchmark evaluates tool calls, it provides limited insights into how well the LLM reasons *about* the graph data before and after tool usage. Does providing graph algorithms as tools truly enhance reasoning, or does it simply offload the task entirely?
    * **Dependency on Neo4j/GDS:**  The reliance on Neo4j and its GDS library limits generalizability. While Neo4j is a popular graph database, the framework might not be directly applicable to other graph database technologies or in-memory graph representations.
    * **Lack of ablation/comparison:** It is unclear how it performs against strong baselines like graph transformers or pure-LLM textualization approaches (especially on the new benchmark).
    * **Failure Mode Analysis:** While the paper acknowledges failures, the analysis could be more in-depth. The reliance on simple metrics like "line capacity" to explain the LLM's flawed reasoning feels shallow. There's not enough exploration of *why* the LLM makes incorrect assumptions or fails to plan effectively. A more formal error analysis would strengthen the findings.
    * **Open-ended tasks analysis still at a high level:**  It wasn't very clear why the agent sometimes would ignore some of the instructions in the original prompts.

* **Potential Impact:** The GDS Agent has the potential to influence research in areas of:
    * **LLM Tool Usage:**  It provides a concrete example of how to effectively integrate domain-specific tools into LLM workflows.
    * **Graph Reasoning:** It can stimulate further exploration into how to equip LLMs with better graph reasoning capabilities.
    * **Benchmark Development:** The benchmark can serve as a starting point for developing more comprehensive evaluation metrics for graph-based LLM agents.

* **Justification for Score:**
While the paper presents a valuable system and benchmark, the limited evaluation, strong reliance on a specific technology stack, and the surface-level failure mode analysis limit its overall impact. The lack of a rigorous comparison against existing approaches prevents a clear assessment of its true performance gain. The findings are promising, but require more thorough investigation.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### CraftGraffiti: Exploring Human Identity with Custom Graffiti Art via Facial-Preserving Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20640v1)
- **Authors**: Ayan Banerjee, Fernando Vilariño, Josep Lladós
- **Abstract**: Preserving facial identity under extreme stylistic transformation remains a major challenge in generative art. In graffiti, a high-contrast, abstract medium, subtle distortions to the eyes, nose, or mouth can erase the subject's recognizability, undermining both personal and cultural authenticity. We present CraftGraffiti, an end-to-end text-guided graffiti generation framework designed with facial feature preservation as a primary objective. Given an input image and a style and pose descriptive prompt, CraftGraffiti first applies graffiti style transfer via LoRA-fine-tuned pretrained diffusion transformer, then enforces identity fidelity through a face-consistent self-attention mechanism that augments attention layers with explicit identity embeddings. Pose customization is achieved without keypoints, using CLIP-guided prompt extension to enable dynamic re-posing while retaining facial coherence. We formally justify and empirically validate the "style-first, identity-after" paradigm, showing it reduces attribute drift compared to the reverse order. Quantitative results demonstrate competitive facial feature consistency and state-of-the-art aesthetic and human preference scores, while qualitative analyses and a live deployment at the Cruilla Festival highlight the system's real-world creative impact. CraftGraffiti advances the goal of identity-respectful AI-assisted artistry, offering a principled approach for blending stylistic freedom with recognizability in creative AI applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CraftGraffiti: Exploring Human Identity with Custom Graffiti Art via Facial-Preserving Diffusion Models."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CraftGraffiti, a novel end-to-end text-guided framework designed to generate custom graffiti-style portraits while preserving the facial identity of the input subject. It addresses the challenge of maintaining recognizability during extreme stylistic transformations, specifically in the context of graffiti art where subtle distortions can erase the subject's identity. CraftGraffiti combines a LoRA-fine-tuned diffusion transformer for style transfer, a face-consistent self-attention mechanism to enforce identity fidelity, and CLIP-guided prompt extension for pose customization. The paper formally justifies the "style-first, identity-after" paradigm and validates it empirically. The system was also deployed in a real-world scenario at a music festival in Barcelona.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents several novel aspects:

*   **Integrated Framework:** The combination of style transfer, identity preservation, and pose customization in a single framework is a significant contribution. While individual components exist, their synergistic integration for graffiti art generation is novel.
*   **Face-Consistent Self-Attention:**  The design and implementation of a self-attention module specifically for maintaining facial consistency within the diffusion process is a crucial and innovative element. It effectively biases attention towards features that match the identity embedding.
*   **'Style-First, Identity-After' Justification:** The formal justification and empirical validation of this paradigm provide valuable insights for generative art.
*   **Real-World Deployment:** Deploying the system at a public festival (Cruïlla) provides real-world insights into usability, user feedback, and system performance in uncontrolled conditions.

However, each of these is a smaller novelty on top of pre-existing frameworks. Style transfer and LoRA-fine-tuned diffusion models exist, and face-preserving methods have been tried before. The integration and focus on graffiti are the main selling points, but it relies heavily on existing tech.

**Significance:**

*   **Addresses Identity Preservation Challenge:** The paper tackles a crucial challenge in generative AI – preserving identity across stylistic transformations – which is vital for ethical and practical applications.
*   **Promotes Inclusive Art Generation:**  The framework has the potential to democratize street art and allow for more personalized cultural expression.
*   **Provides Insights into Identity-Respectful AI:** The paper contributes to the growing field of identity-respectful AI by offering a principled approach to blending stylistic freedom with recognizability.
*   **Applications Beyond Graffiti:** The techniques developed here could have broader applications in other image generation and editing tasks where identity preservation is important.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of identity preservation in graffiti art generation.
*   **Comprehensive Approach:**  CraftGraffiti offers a complete end-to-end solution integrating several components.
*   **Empirical Validation:** Quantitative results, qualitative analyses, and a real-world deployment demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach.
*   **Well-Written and Structured:**  The paper is well-organized and easy to follow.
*   **Ethical considerations** are at least mentioned, although they could be explored in more depth.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Comparison:** The paper's quantitative comparison to state-of-the-art image editing techniques might not be entirely fair. It's difficult to compare the preservation of facial features since the other models are unable to achieve style transfer.  Ablation studies provide more relevant analysis.
*   **Computational Cost:** The high computational cost might limit its widespread adoption, especially for real-time applications (as stated).
*   **Limited Cultural Scope:** The evaluation is conducted in a single cultural context (European music festival), which may limit the generalizability of the findings.
*   **Limited detail on societal impact:** Despite mentioning it, the ethical considerations and potential harms of these types of technologies aren't explored in depth.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper's contributions could influence research in several areas: identity-preserving image generation, style transfer, personalized art generation, and AI-assisted cultural expression.  The face-consistent self-attention mechanism could be adapted for other tasks where maintaining fine-grained details is critical. The real-world deployment offers valuable lessons for designing and evaluating generative AI systems in public spaces.

**Score:**

Score: 7

**Justification:**

CraftGraffiti addresses a niche but important problem in generative art, combining several techniques in a way that maintains facial consistency during style transfer. The novelty comes more from the integration and specific application to graffiti art rather than groundbreaking individual innovations. The real-world deployment is a significant strength. Despite some limitations in the comparison to state-of-the-art methods and areas where more detail would've enhanced the paper, the overall contribution is significant and addresses an under-explored problem. The contribution definitely shows a reasonable amount of innovation within a fairly active subfield of computer vision.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### VarDiU: A Variational Diffusive Upper Bound for One-Step Diffusion Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20646v1)
- **Authors**: Leyang Wang, Mingtian Zhang, Zijing Ou, David Barber
- **Abstract**: Recently, diffusion distillation methods have compressed thousand-step teacher diffusion models into one-step student generators while preserving sample quality. Most existing approaches train the student model using a diffusive divergence whose gradient is approximated via the student's score function, learned through denoising score matching (DSM). Since DSM training is imperfect, the resulting gradient estimate is inevitably biased, leading to sub-optimal performance. In this paper, we propose VarDiU (pronounced /va:rdju:/), a Variational Diffusive Upper Bound that admits an unbiased gradient estimator and can be directly applied to diffusion distillation. Using this objective, we compare our method with Diff-Instruct and demonstrate that it achieves higher generation quality and enables a more efficient and stable training procedure for one-step diffusion distillation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "VarDiU: A Variational Diffusive Upper Bound for One-Step Diffusion Distillation" introduces a new method for distilling diffusion models into one-step generative models. The core idea is to formulate a variational upper bound on the diffusive divergence used to train the student model. This upper bound admits an unbiased gradient estimator, addressing the bias inherent in existing methods (like Diff-Instruct) that rely on denoising score matching (DSM) for gradient approximation. The paper demonstrates empirically that VarDiU achieves higher generation quality and more stable training compared to Diff-Instruct on a toy 2D mixture of Gaussians dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the formulation of a variational upper bound for diffusive divergence that permits unbiased gradient estimation.  While variational upper bounds are common techniques in machine learning, their application to diffusion distillation with the specific aim of addressing bias in score function estimation is a valuable contribution. The identification and mitigation of bias in DSM-based training for diffusion distillation is a significant improvement.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its potential to improve the efficiency and stability of diffusion distillation.  One-step diffusion models are highly desirable for their speed, but their training can be challenging. By reducing bias in the gradient estimation, VarDiU aims to make this process more robust and reliable. The empirical results, while limited to a simple dataset, support this claim, suggesting that VarDiU can achieve better performance with fewer training steps than existing methods.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition: The paper clearly identifies the issue of biased gradient estimates in DSM-based diffusion distillation.
    *   Sound theoretical justification: The derivation of the variational upper bound is well-motivated and mathematically sound.
    *   Empirical validation: The paper provides empirical evidence to support the effectiveness of VarDiU on a toy dataset, demonstrating improved performance and stability.
    *   The observation that the joint entropy is constant, thereby simplifying the upper bound calculation, is a valuable technical contribution.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited empirical evaluation: The experiments are confined to a simple 2D mixture of Gaussians.  The performance on more complex, high-dimensional datasets (e.g., images) is not demonstrated. This limits the generalizability of the conclusions.
    *   Lack of comparison with other distillation methods: The comparison is primarily against Diff-Instruct. While Diff-Instruct is a relevant baseline, it would be beneficial to compare against a broader range of distillation techniques, especially recent advances, to fully assess the advantages of VarDiU.
    *   Computational complexity: The paper does not thoroughly discuss the computational overhead of estimating the unbiased gradient compared to the biased gradient in DSM. While the unbiased estimation promises more stable training, it is vital to consider any potential increase in computational cost.
    * While the observation of constant joint entropy is notable, the improvement it provides is constrained.

*   **Potential Impact:** If the benefits of VarDiU translate to higher-dimensional and more complex datasets, it could become a valuable tool for training efficient diffusion models.  The unbiased gradient estimation technique could inspire further research into mitigating bias in other score-based generative models. The connection to information maximization (IM) provides another angle that can be further explored.

**Score:** 6

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel approach to address a key challenge in diffusion distillation – the bias in gradient estimation. The theoretical justification and initial empirical results are promising. However, the limited scope of the experiments and the lack of comparison with other state-of-the-art distillation methods prevent a higher score. Also, the significance of joint entropy removal is minor. While the technique is well-motivated and offers a potential improvement, its true impact will depend on its performance in more realistic settings. The paper represents a solid contribution, but further research is needed to fully validate its effectiveness and impact on the field. The limitations of not having complex datasets and limited comparison to other distillation methods lead to a score of 6.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Improving Alignment in LVLMs with Debiased Self-Judgment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20655v1)
- **Authors**: Sihan Yang, Chenhang Cui, Zihao Zhao, Yiyang Zhou, Weilong Yan, Ying Wei, Huaxiu Yao
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) and Large Visual-Language Models (LVLMs) have opened up new opportunities for integrating visual and linguistic modalities. However, effectively aligning these modalities remains challenging, often leading to hallucinations--where generated outputs are not grounded in the visual input--and raising safety concerns across various domains. Existing alignment methods, such as instruction tuning and preference tuning, often rely on external datasets, human annotations, or complex post-processing, which limit scalability and increase costs. To address these challenges, we propose a novel approach that generates the debiased self-judgment score, a self-evaluation metric created internally by the model without relying on external resources. This enables the model to autonomously improve alignment. Our method enhances both decoding strategies and preference tuning processes, resulting in reduced hallucinations, enhanced safety, and improved overall capability. Empirical results show that our approach significantly outperforms traditional methods, offering a more effective solution for aligning LVLMs.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces a novel approach to improve the alignment of Large Visual-Language Models (LVLMs) by using a "debiased self-judgment score." This score, generated internally by the model, evaluates the faithfulness of the generated output without relying on external resources or human annotations. The method mitigates the bias towards the textual modality, which is inherent in LVLMs due to their reliance on Large Language Models (LLMs). The paper integrates this debiased self-judgment score into both decoding strategies (Debiased Self-Guided Decoding - DSGD) to reduce hallucinations and preference tuning (Debiased Self-Rewarding - DSR) to improve overall capabilities and safety. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach in reducing hallucinations, enhancing safety, and improving performance on various benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper's novelty lies in its self-supervised approach to aligning LVLMs. Most existing methods rely on external data, human annotation, or other models, which increases costs, limits scalability, and introduces potential biases. The proposed approach, by leveraging internal model capabilities, addresses these limitations. The concept of using the model's intrinsic confidence and debiasing it to improve alignment is indeed a novel contribution.

The significance of this work is its potential to enable more autonomous and scalable alignment of LVLMs. In a future where AI models surpass human capabilities, self-evaluation and self-improvement will be crucial. This paper presents a significant step in that direction, demonstrating a feasible path towards achieving alignment without external supervision.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The self-supervised alignment method with debiased self-judgment is novel.
*   **Effectiveness:** The proposed method demonstrates significant improvements in reducing hallucinations, enhancing safety, and improving overall capabilities.
*   **Scalability:** The approach avoids reliance on external data, human annotations, or complex post-processing, making it scalable.
*   **Thorough Evaluation:** The paper presents extensive experimental results on diverse benchmarks, showcasing the robustness of the approach.
*   **Clear and Well-written:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the methodology and experimental setup.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Accessibility:** The method relies on accessing the model's token logits, which may not be available in all models (e.g., closed-source models like GPT-4). This limits the applicability of the proposed approach.
*   **Experimental Scope:** The experiments are limited to a few common LVLMs. Further validation on a wider range of models would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
*   **Language Dependence:** The jailbreak attack experiments were conducted only in English. The effectiveness of the approach in other languages needs to be evaluated.
*   **Bias:** While text modality bias is addressed, the model might suffer from biases in pre-training data.

**Impact and Influence:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of LVLM alignment. It opens up new avenues for research in self-supervised alignment techniques. It provides a valuable framework for leveraging internal model capabilities to improve faithfulness, safety, and overall performance. Future research can build upon this work to develop more sophisticated self-evaluation mechanisms and address the limitations mentioned above.

Score: 8

Justification: The paper presents a novel and effective approach to LVLM alignment, addressing critical challenges in the field. The self-supervised nature of the method and its demonstrated improvements in hallucinations and safety are significant contributions. However, the limitations regarding accessibility, experimental scope, language dependence, and potential for other biases slightly reduce the overall score. The impact on the field is likely to be considerable, inspiring further research in this direction.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### CodecBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Acoustic and Semantic Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20660v1)
- **Authors**: Ruifan Deng, Yitian Gong, Qinghui Gao, Luozhijie Jin, Qinyuan Cheng, Zhaoye Fei, Shimin Li, Xipeng Qiu
- **Abstract**: With the rise of multimodal large language models (LLMs), audio codec plays an increasingly vital role in encoding audio into discrete tokens, enabling integration of audio into text-based LLMs. Current audio codec captures two types of information: acoustic and semantic. As audio codec is applied to diverse scenarios in speech language model , it needs to model increasingly complex information and adapt to varied contexts, such as scenarios with multiple speakers, background noise, or richer paralinguistic information. However, existing codec's own evaluation has been limited by simplistic metrics and scenarios, and existing benchmarks for audio codec are not designed for complex application scenarios, which limits the assessment performance on complex datasets for acoustic and semantic capabilities. We introduce CodecBench, a comprehensive evaluation dataset to assess audio codec performance from both acoustic and semantic perspectives across four data domains. Through this benchmark, we aim to identify current limitations, highlight future research directions, and foster advances in the development of audio codec. The codes are available at https://github.com/RayYuki/CodecBench.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CodecBench paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CodecBench, a new benchmark for evaluating audio codecs, specifically tailored for their use in speech language models (LLMs). It addresses the limitations of existing benchmarks, which often use simplistic metrics and datasets lacking real-world complexities like noise, multilingual settings, and emotional variability. CodecBench features a diverse collection of datasets spanning speech, music, sound, and general audio, along with a comprehensive set of acoustic and semantic evaluation metrics. The semantic evaluation includes an ASR probing task to assess alignment with text and a classification task to evaluate the preservation of contextual and emotional information. The paper presents experimental results comparing several popular audio codecs using the CodecBench framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The primary novelty of the paper lies in its comprehensive approach to evaluating audio codecs in the context of LLMs. While individual aspects like using diverse datasets or specific evaluation metrics might not be entirely new, the combination of these elements and the specific focus on real-world audio complexities represents a significant advancement. The inclusion of semantic metrics, especially the classification task focused on emotional and contextual information, is a valuable addition beyond traditional acoustic evaluations. Furthermore, the self-collected general audio dataset, designed with exaggerated expressiveness and complex speaker scenarios, addresses a recognized gap in existing benchmarks.

**Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its potential to drive progress in audio codec development for LLM applications. By providing a more rigorous and comprehensive evaluation framework, CodecBench can help researchers and practitioners identify the strengths and weaknesses of different codecs and guide the design of new codecs that better handle complex audio inputs. The analysis of popular codec models reveals critical problems on datasets, providing valuable insights into how performance is affected by parameters of the audio codecs. The insights derived from CodecBench can inform choices about which codec is most appropriate for a particular application.  By bridging the evaluation gap, the paper enables better assessment of semantic alignment with text.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** CodecBench provides a multifaceted evaluation framework, combining diverse datasets, acoustic metrics, and semantic metrics.
*   **Real-World Focus:** The benchmark emphasizes real-world audio complexities, addressing a key limitation of existing benchmarks.
*   **Semantic Evaluation:**  The inclusion of semantic metrics, including the ASR probing and classification tasks, is a significant contribution.
*   **Clear Structure and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the motivation, methodology, and experimental results.
*   **Accessible Code and Data:** The availability of code and data promotes reproducibility and facilitates further research.
*   **Detailed Analysis:** The extensive analysis and insights provided based on the experiments with popular codec models is valuable.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Explanation of Self-Collected Data:** The details surrounding the self-collected dataset could be more detailed.  Specifically, the process for creating and curating this dataset could be expanded.
*   **Evaluation Scope:** While more comprehensive than other benchmarks, the limitations section admits to shortcomings in addressing extreme scenarios, requiring future enhancements to testing methodologies.
*   **ASR Implementation:** the implementation of an ASR evaluation metric is relatively simple. Given the vast space of language models available, a more robust method of evaluation might be preferred.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 8 to this paper. While the individual components of CodecBench are not entirely novel, the combination of these components, the clear focus on speech language models and real-world scenarios, and the inclusion of semantic evaluation metrics constitute a significant advancement. The insights into the performance of popular codecs are valuable, and the open availability of the benchmark promotes further research and development. Although the paper admits certain limitations requiring further exploration, its contributions represent a valuable advancement in the field of audio codec evaluation and are expected to influence future research directions.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 8/10

### Amadeus: Autoregressive Model with Bidirectional Attribute Modelling for Symbolic Music
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20665v1)
- **Authors**: Hongju Su, Ke Li, Lan Yang, Honggang Zhang, Yi-Zhe Song
- **Abstract**: Existing state-of-the-art symbolic music generation models predominantly adopt autoregressive or hierarchical autoregressive architectures, modelling symbolic music as a sequence of attribute tokens with unidirectional temporal dependencies, under the assumption of a fixed, strict dependency structure among these attributes. However, we observe that using different attributes as the initial token in these models leads to comparable performance. This suggests that the attributes of a musical note are, in essence, a concurrent and unordered set, rather than a temporally dependent sequence. Based on this insight, we introduce Amadeus, a novel symbolic music generation framework. Amadeus adopts a two-level architecture: an autoregressive model for note sequences and a bidirectional discrete diffusion model for attributes. To enhance performance, we propose Music Latent Space Discriminability Enhancement Strategy(MLSDES), incorporating contrastive learning constraints that amplify discriminability of intermediate music representations. The Conditional Information Enhancement Module (CIEM) simultaneously strengthens note latent vector representation via attention mechanisms, enabling more precise note decoding. We conduct extensive experiments on unconditional and text-conditioned generation tasks. Amadeus significantly outperforms SOTA models across multiple metrics while achieving at least 4$\times$ speed-up. Furthermore, we demonstrate training-free, fine-grained note attribute control feasibility using our model. To explore the upper performance bound of the Amadeus architecture, we compile the largest open-source symbolic music dataset to date, AMD (Amadeus MIDI Dataset), supporting both pre-training and fine-tuning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Amadeus: Autoregressive Model with Bidirectional Attribute Modelling for Symbolic Music":

**Summary:**
